USER_TASK_PROMPT:
Design a skill for forecasting a land parcel's satellite band values with the synthetic historical control method of Chen, Yang and Yang. The paper, Synthetic Historical Control for Policy Evaluation, is at `paper/ssrn-4995085.pdf`. The agent that uses the skill works in a directory holding `data.csv`, one parcel's own history of several bands, one row per band and time point. It predicts every band's value at the time point after that history and writes `answer.json` as {"prediction": {"<band>": <number>, ...}, "rationale": "<text>"}. The agent has a bash shell and no network access.

SKILL_QUALITY_STANDARDS:
Every skill created or significantly updated in this stage MUST satisfy ALL of the following requirements.
These are not optional guidelines—they are hard requirements for skill acceptance.

1. ACTIONABLE RUNBOOK — SKILL.md must be an operator-grade execution playbook, not a high-level summary.
   - The Procedure section must contain numbered, concrete steps that an agent can execute directly.
   - Each step must specify:
       * The exact command or Python/shell snippet to run (not a placeholder like "run the script").
       * What intermediate file or output is produced and its key fields or shape.
       * How to inspect and validate the result at this checkpoint before proceeding to the next step.
       * The decision logic to apply based on the observed result ("if X then Y, else Z").
   - Include at least one realistic, non-trivial usage example that produces scientifically meaningful
     output—not just a smoke-test or toy placeholder.
   - If the skill wraps an external API, document: endpoint URL, authentication method,
     rate limits, and a concrete retry strategy with example code.
   - If the skill requires searching for data or references, provide explicit search guidance:
       * Which databases, search engines, or repositories to query (e.g., PubMed, OpenAlex, PyPI,
         GitHub, CELLxGENE, UniProt, NCBI, etc.).
       * How to formulate effective queries with concrete example query strings.
       * How to evaluate, rank, and select search results (what metadata to inspect, what criteria
         to prefer, when to stop searching).
       * How to download and verify the selected resource before using it.
   - If the skill uses specific tools or libraries, show the concrete API usage:
       * Import statements and initialization code.
       * Key function calls with realistic argument values, not just `func(args)` placeholders.
       * How to interpret the return values and handle edge cases (empty results, pagination, etc.).
   - Separate the real-run workflow clearly from any deterministic CI/smoke-test path.

2. ENVIRONMENT SETUP — dedicate a named section in SKILL.md titled "Environment Setup" (or "Requirements").
   - List every required package with its installation command:
       pip install <package>==<version>   # or conda equivalent
   - State the minimum runtime version (Python ≥ X.Y, R ≥ X.Y, etc.) and any known version conflicts.
   - Describe any environment variables or config files that must be set before running.
   - Provide a "pre-flight check" the agent can run to verify the environment is ready, e.g.:
       python3 -c "import scanpy; print(scanpy.__version__)"
   - When a required package has version constraints (e.g., Python < 3.13), provide a working
     environment-creation recipe:
       conda create -n skill-env python=3.11 && conda activate skill-env && pip install ...
   - Include a browser-based or offline fallback path when the primary package cannot be installed.
   - If the skill depends on external CLI tools (e.g., bcftools, GATK, samtools, nextflow),
     document how to install and verify them:
       which bcftools && bcftools --version

3. ERROR HANDLING — dedicate a named section in SKILL.md titled "Troubleshooting" or "Error Handling".
   - Cover the 3–5 most common failure modes (network timeout, missing package, bad input format,
     API auth failure, disk space, unexpected tool version, etc.).
   - For each failure mode state:
       * The recognizable symptom: exact error message, exit code, or observable behavior.
       * A concrete, step-by-step remediation procedure.
       * An alternate execution path or manual fallback when the primary tool is unavailable.
   - Scripts themselves must handle errors gracefully: catch exceptions, emit a descriptive message
     to stderr, and exit with a non-zero code—never silent failures or bare `except: pass`.

4. PATH PORTABILITY — no hardcoded absolute or repo-anchored paths anywhere in SKILL.md or scripts.
   - SKILL.md procedures and examples MUST NOT contain absolute paths (e.g., /Users/... /home/...).
   - SKILL.md MUST NOT embed paths anchored to this specific repository layout.
       BAD:  python3 skills/genomics/bcftools-variant-filtering-starter/scripts/run_bcftools.py
       BAD:  --input skills/genomics/bcftools-variant-filtering-starter/examples/toy.vcf
       GOOD: python3 scripts/run_bcftools.py              # documented as: run from the skill directory
       GOOD: python3 run_bcftools.py                      # or: add the scripts/ dir to PATH
       GOOD: python3 <skill_dir>/scripts/run_bcftools.py  # generic placeholder form
   - All scripts must accept every input/output path as a command-line argument
     (--input, --out, --workspace, etc.); never hardcode file paths inside script source code.
   - Acceptable exception: test_commands in metadata.yaml may use the canonical
     `skills/<domain>/<slug>/scripts/...` form relative to the repository root, since the
     framework always runs them from the repo root. This exception does NOT extend to SKILL.md.

5. SCRIPT DESIGN — every helper script in the skill's scripts/ directory must:
   - Be self-contained: import only standard-library or well-known third-party packages
     (declared in Environment Setup); do not import from other skills or internal framework modules.
   - Accept ALL file paths (input data, output files, working directory, config) via command-line
     arguments with argparse or equivalent. Provide sensible defaults only when portable
     (e.g., default output to current directory, not a hardcoded repo path).
   - Include a --help flag that documents every argument, its type, and a short usage example.
   - Print a JSON summary on stdout (or write to --out) so downstream automation can parse results.
   - Exit with code 0 on success, non-zero on failure; write diagnostics to stderr.
   - Handle common edge cases: empty input, missing network, oversized files, unexpected types.
   - Use an `if __name__ == "__main__":` guard so the script can also be imported as a module.

6. COMPLETENESS — verify SKILL.md contains ALL of these named sections before finishing:
   [ ] Purpose — 1–2 sentence functional description and primary use case
   [ ] When to use / When not to use — precise boundary conditions and routing to adjacent skills
   [ ] Environment Setup — packages, versions, pre-flight check, environment recipe if needed
   [ ] Inputs — each input with: name, type/format, whether required or optional, example value
   [ ] Outputs — each output with: filename pattern, format, key fields, and size/shape guidance
   [ ] Procedure — numbered steps with exact commands, expected artifacts, and decision checkpoints
   [ ] Troubleshooting / Error Handling — top failure modes with symptoms and remediation
   [ ] Validation — how to confirm a successful end-to-end run, including expected output shape
   [ ] Related Skills — cross-references to adjacent or prerequisite skills in the repository

- Literature search

- Benchmark-paper mining
- Method-section extraction

- Scientific summarization

- Metadata harmonization
- Format conversion

# DoWhy Average Treatment Effect Starter

Use this skill to estimate a deterministic toy average treatment effect with DoWhy and summarize a simple placebo refutation.

## What it does

- Builds a toy observational dataset with one confounder, one binary treatment, and one continuous outcome.
- Identifies the backdoor estimand with DoWhy.
- Estimates the average treatment effect with linear regression.
- Runs a placebo-treatment refutation and returns compact JSON.

## When to use it

- You need a minimal local starter for causal-inference workflows in Python.
- You want a verified example of identification, estimation, and refutation with DoWhy.

## Example

```bash
slurm/envs/causal/bin/python skills/statistical-and-machine-learning-foundations-for-science/dowhy-average-treatment-effect-starter/scripts/run_dowhy_average_treatment_effect.py \

"""End-to-end Synthetic Historical Control procedure (Chen-Yang-Yang 2024).

Composes the estimator's stages (Section 2.3) using the shared numerical
kernels in :mod:`mlsynth.utils.shc_helpers.kernels` and
:mod:`mlsynth.utils.datautils`:

    1. LOOCV-select a kernel bandwidth and smooth the pre-period into the
       latent trend ``ell_hat`` (Eq. 21).
    2. Build the treated block and the overlapping historical blocks
       (Eq. 7).
    3. Match the treated pre-segment with a simplex combination of *all*
       historical pre-segments by solving the nearest-PD QP (Eq. 23).
    4. (Optional) augment with a ridge refinement (ASHC).
    5. Apply the weights to the historical forward segments to obtain the
       post-intervention counterfactual.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

def main() -> int:
    skills = load_jsonl(REGISTRY / "skills.jsonl")
    resources = load_jsonl(REGISTRY / "resources_dedup.jsonl")
    taxonomy = load_json(REGISTRY / "taxonomy.yaml")

    skill_entries = []
    for skill in skills:
        entry = dict(skill)
        skill_path = ROOT / skill["path"]
        entry["source_resource_count"] = len(skill.get("source_resource_ids", []))
        entry["links"] = {
            "skill": f"{skill['path']}/SKILL.md",
            "metadata": f"{skill['path']}/metadata.yaml",
            "refs": f"{skill['path']}/refs.md",
            "examples": f"{skill['path']}/examples",
            "tests": f"{skill['path']}/tests",
        }
        entry["file_count"] = sum(1 for _ in skill_path.rglob("*") if _.is_file())
        skill_entries.append(entry)

"""Run the repository smoke target for every registered skill and summarize the matrix."""

def choose_target(skill: dict, smoke_map: dict[str, list[str]]) -> str | None:
    targets = smoke_map.get(skill["skill_id"], [])
    if not targets:
        return None
    return sorted(targets)[0]

def classify_reason(test: unittest.TestCase, tb: str) -> str:
    """Extract a short reason from a traceback."""
    last_line = tb.strip().splitlines()[-1] if tb.strip() else "Unknown"

        for line in tb.splitlines():
            if "No such file or directory" in line:
                path = line.split("No such file or directory:")[-1].strip().strip("'\"")

# Protein Language Model Function Analysis Starter

Use this skill to validate protein FASTA inputs, extract deterministic smoke-safe embeddings, and run a reusable sequence-to-function triage flow that can later be swapped onto real ESM-2 or ProtT5 backends.

## What This Skill Does

- reads protein sequences from FASTA and rejects unsupported residue symbols
- emits per-sequence embeddings to a TSV contract
- runs centroid-based function analysis when labels are supplied
- reports nearest-neighbor structure in embedding space
- preserves a stable CLI for real `transformers` backends such as ESM-2 and ProtT5

## When To Use It

- when you need a concrete local skill for `protein-embeddings` and `sequence-to-function-modeling`
- when you want a deterministic smoke path before moving to GPU-backed protein language model inference
- when you need a compact handoff artifact for downstream DeepFRI, annotation, or benchmarking work

exec
/usr/bin/bash -lc "cat > /data/wang/junh/githubs/latent-synthetic-control/Satellite/notebooks/skillfoundry_profile/work/deepseek/skills/statistical-and-machine-learning-foundations-for-science/synthetic-historical-control-band-forecast/scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py <<'PYEOF'
#"'!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Forecast a parcel'"'s next-time-point satellite band values with the
Synthetic Historical Control (SHC) method of Chen, Yang & Yang.

The SHC method reuses one unit's own distant history as a donor pool. A
\"treated block\" is the most recent "'``m`` observations; each "historical
block" is an older window that mirrors the treated block'"'s pre/post split.
The treated pre-segment is matched with a convex (simplex) combination of
historical pre-segments; those same weights are applied to the historical
\"pseudo-post\" segments to build the counterfactual, i.e. the forecast.

This script is self-contained and uses only the Python standard library so
it runs offline on any machine with the agent's bash shell. It:

  1. reads a "'``data.csv`` describing one parcel'"'s band history,
  2. optionally detrends and standardizes each band,
  3. estimates the latent (smooth) component with local-linear kernel
     regression whose bandwidth is chosen by leave-one-out cross validation,
  4. matches the treated block to historical blocks via a simplex-constrained
     least-squares problem (projected-gradient solver),
  5. writes "'``answer.json`` with ``{"prediction": {...}, "rationale": ...}``
     and prints a machine-readable JSON summary.

See SKILL.md for the full runbook, environment setup, and troubleshooting.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import json
import math
import statistics
import sys
from pathlib import Path

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Data loading and format detection
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

BAND_KEYS = ("band", "band_name", "bandname", "band_id", "channel", "variable", "var")
TIME_KEYS = ("time", "date", "timestamp", "t", "period", "datetime", "index")
VALUE_KEYS = ("value", "reflectance", "band_value", "val", "y", "measurement")


def _column_lookup(fieldnames: list[str]) -> dict[str, str | None]:
    lowered = {name.lower().strip(): name for name in fieldnames}
    found: dict[str, str | None] = {"band": None, "time": None, "value": None}
    for key, target in (("band", BAND_KEYS), ("time", TIME_KEYS), ("value", VALUE_KEYS)):
        for candidate in target:
            if candidate in lowered:
                found[key] = lowered[candidate]
                break
    return found


def _to_float(raw: str) -> float | None:
    if raw is None:
        return None
    text = str(raw).strip()
    if not text or text.lower() in {"na", "nan", "none", "null", "."}:
        return None
    try:
        return float(text)
    except ValueError:
        return None


def load_long(rows: list[dict[str, str]], cols: dict[str, str | None]) -> dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]]:
    series: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {}
    time_as_str: dict[str, dict[float, float]] = {}
    seq_counter = 0
    for row in rows:
        band = str(row[cols["band"]]).strip()
        value = _to_float(row[cols["value"]])
        if not band or value is None:
            continue
        raw_time = str(row[cols["time"]]).strip()
        parsed = _to_float(raw_time)
        if parsed is None:
            # Non-numeric timestamps: assign a stable order of first appearance.
            if raw_time not in time_as_str.setdefault(band, {}):
                seq_counter += 1
                time_as_str[band][raw_time] = float(seq_counter)
            t = time_as_str[band][raw_time]
        else:
            t = parsed
        series.setdefault(band, []).append((t, value))
    return series


def load_wide(rows: list[dict[str, str]], fieldnames: list[str]) -> dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]]:
    """Wide format: either rows are time points and columns are bands, or the
    first column is the band and remaining columns are time points."""
    first = fieldnames[0]
    first_is_band = first.lower().strip() in BAND_KEYS
    series: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {}

    if not first_is_band:
        # rows = time, columns = bands
        for row in rows:
            t = _to_float(row[first])
            if t is None:
                continue
            for band in fieldnames[1:]:
                value = _to_float(row[band])
                if value is None:
                    continue
                series.setdefault(band, []).append((t, value))
    else:
        # rows = bands, columns = time points
        for row in rows:
            band = str(row[first]).strip()
            if not band:
                continue
            for idx, time_col in enumerate(fieldnames[1:]):
                value = _to_float(row[time_col])
                if value is None:
                    continue
                t = _to_float(time_col)
                t = t if t is not None else float(idx)
                series.setdefault(band, []).append((t, value))
    return series


def load_series(path: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]]:
    with path.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        reader = csv.DictReader(handle)
        if reader.fieldnames is None:
            raise ValueError(f"{path} has no header row")
        fieldnames = [name for name in reader.fieldnames if name is not None]
        rows = list(reader)

    if not rows:
        raise ValueError(f"{path} has no data rows")

    cols = _column_lookup(fieldnames)
    if cols["band"] and cols["value"]:
        series = load_long(rows, cols)
        # If the long parser found a single band with duplicated time stamps it
        # is more likely a wide table with an unusual header.
        if len(series) >= 1 and any(len(v) > 1 for v in series.values()):
            return _sort_series(series)
    return _sort_series(load_wide(rows, fieldnames))


def _sort_series(series: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]]) -> dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]]:
    cleaned: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {}
    for band, points in series.items():
        if not points:
            continue
        # Average any duplicate time stamps, then sort ascending.
        buckets: dict[float, list[float]] = {}
        for t, value in points:
            buckets.setdefault(t, []).append(value)
        ordered = sorted((t, sum(vals) / len(vals)) for t, vals in buckets.items())
        cleaned[band] = ordered
    return cleaned


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Numerical helpers (pure Python)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def gaussian_kernel(u: float) -> float:
    return math.exp(-0.5 * u * u)


def local_linear_predict(
    xs: list[float],
    ys: list[float],
    h_abs: float,
    targets: list[float],
    exclude_index: int | None = None,
) -> list[float]:
    """Local-linear (degree-1) kernel smoother evaluated at ``targets``.

    ``h_abs`` is the bandwidth in the same units as ``xs``. Points far from the
    target simply receive negligible Gaussian weight. If the local design is
    singular this falls back to a weighted mean.
    """
    preds: list[float] = []
    for xt in targets:
        s0 = s1 = s2 = t0 = t1 = 0.0
        for idx, (x, y) in enumerate(zip(xs, ys)):
            if exclude_index is not None and idx == exclude_index:
                continue
            z = (x - xt) / h_abs
            if abs(z) > 8.0:
                continue
            w = gaussian_kernel(z)
            if w <= 0.0:
                continue
            s0 += w
            s1 += w * z
            s2 += w * z * z
            t0 += w * y
            t1 += w * z * y
        if s0 <= 0.0:
            preds.append(float("nan"))
            continue
        det = s0 * s2 - s1 * s1
        if abs(det) < 1e-12:
            preds.append(t0 / s0)
            continue
        a = (t0 * s2 - t1 * s1) / det
        preds.append(a)
    return preds


def select_bandwidth(xs: list[float], ys: list[float], grid: list[float]) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Pick the bandwidth minimizing leave-one-out CV error. Returns (h_abs, cv)."""
    n = len(xs)
    if n < 3:
        return (max(1.0, float(n)), float("inf"))
    best_h = grid[0]
    best_cv = float("inf")
    for h_frac in grid:
        h_abs = max(h_frac * n, 1e-6)
        errors: list[float] = []
        for i in range(n):
            pred = local_linear_predict(xs, ys, h_abs, [xs[i]], exclude_index=i)[0]
            if math.isnan(pred):
                continue
            errors.append((pred - ys[i]) ** 2)
        if not errors:
            continue
        cv = sum(errors) / len(errors)
        if cv < best_cv:
            best_cv = cv
            best_h = h_abs
    return best_h, best_cv


def project_simplex(v: list[float]) -> list[float]:
    """Euclidean projection of a vector onto the probability simplex."""
    n = len(v)
    if n == 0:
        return []
    u = sorted(v, reverse=True)
    css = 0.0
    rho = 0
    theta = 0.0
    for i, ui in enumerate(u):
        css += ui
        if ui - (css - 1.0) / (i + 1) > 0:
            rho = i + 1
            theta = (css - 1.0) / (i + 1)
    return [max(vi - theta, 0.0) for vi in v]


def solve_simplex_least_squares(
    columns: list[list[float]],
    target: list[float],
    *,
    ridge: float = 1e-6,
    iterations: int = 4000,
) -> list[float]:
    """Solve min_w ||target - sum_i w_i * columns_i||''^2 + ridge*||w||''^2
    subject to w >= 0 and sum(w) = 1 via projected gradient descent.

    ``columns`` is a list of N vectors, each of length m (the pre-segment).
    """
    n = len(columns)
    if n == 0:
        return []
    if n == 1:
        return [1.0]
    m = len(target)

    # Precompute Gram matrix G (N x N) and c = L''^T target (N).
    gram = [[0.0] * n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        ci = columns[i]
        for j in range(i, n):
            cj = columns[j]
            dot = 0.0
            for k in range(m):
                dot += ci[k] * cj[k]
            gram[i][j] = dot
            gram[j][i] = dot
    c = [sum(columns[i][k] * target[k] for k in range(m)) for i in range(n)]

    # Lipschitz bound: ||2(G + ridge I)||_2 <= 2*(max row abs sum + ridge).
    row_sums = [sum(abs(gram[i][j]) for j in range(n)) for i in range(n)]
    lipschitz = 2.0 * (max(row_sums) + ridge)
    if lipschitz <= 0.0:
        return [1.0 / n] * n
    step = 1.0 / lipschitz

    w = [1.0 / n] * n
    for _ in range(iterations):
        grad = [2.0 * (sum(gram[i][j] * w[j] for j in range(n)) + ridge * w[i]) - 2.0 * c[i] for i in range(n)]
        nxt = [w[i] - step * grad[i] for i in range(n)]
        w = project_simplex(nxt)
    return w


def matching_mse(columns: list[list[float]], target: list[float], weights: list[float]) -> float:
    m = len(target)
    if m == 0:
        return float("inf")
    total = 0.0
    for k in range(m):
        approx = sum(weights[i] * columns[i][k] for i in range(len(columns)))
        total += (approx - target[k]) ** 2
    return total / m


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# SHC forecast for a single standardized series
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class SHCConfig:
    def __init__(self, args: argparse.Namespace, n_obs: int) -> None:
        self.donor_mode = args.donor_mode
        self.ridge = args.ridge
        self.bandwidth_grid = args.bandwidth_grid
        self.max_donors = args.max_donors
        self.stepwise_tol = args.stepwise_tol
        self.mse_tol = args.mse_tol
        self.lags = args.lags
        upper = min(args.pre_max, max(3, n_obs - 2))
        lower = min(args.pre_min, upper)
        self.m_min = max(3, lower)
        self.m_max = max(self.m_min, upper)


def _solve_beta(xs: list[list[float]], ys: list[float], mu_x: list[float], mu_y: list[float]) -> list[float]:
    """Least-squares beta from partialling-out residuals (Robinson 1988)."""
    p = len(xs[0]) if xs else 0
    if p == 0:
        return []
    a = [[0.0] * p for _ in range(p)]
    b = [0.0] * p
    for t in range(len(ys)):
        vx = [xs[t][j] - mu_x[j] for j in range(p)]
        vy = ys[t] - mu_y
        for i in range(p):
            b[i] += vx[i] * vy
            for j in range(p):
                a[i][j] += vx[i] * vx[j]
    # Solve a @ beta = b with Gaussian elimination and tiny ridge.
    for i in range(p):
        a[i][i] += 1e-8
    for col in range(p):
        pivot = max(range(col, p), key=lambda r: abs(a[r][col]))
        if abs(a[pivot][col]) < 1e-12:
            continue
        a[col], a[pivot] = a[pivot], a[col]
        b[col], b[pivot] = b[pivot], b[col]
        for r in range(p):
            if r == col:
                continue
            factor = a[r][col] / a[col][col]
            if factor == 0.0:
                continue
            for c in range(col, p):
                a[r][c] -= factor * a[col][c]
            b[r] -= factor * b[col]
    return [b[i] / a[i][i] if abs(a[i][i]) > 1e-12 else 0.0 for i in range(p)]


def forecast_standardized(z: list[float], cfg: SHCConfig) -> dict:
    """Forecast the next value of a standardized series using SHC.

    Returns a diagnostics dictionary with ``forecast`` in standardized units.
    """
    n = len(z)
    diag: dict = {"n_obs": n, "status": "ok", "notes": []}
    if n < 6:
        fallback = z[-1] if z else 0.0
        diag.update({"status": "fallback", "forecast": fallback, "method": "last_value"})
        diag["notes"].append("series shorter than 6 points; used last value")
        return diag

    xs = [float(i) for i in range(n)]

    # Controls: lagged values of the same standardized series.
    lag = cfg.lags
    X: list[list[float]] = []
    y_used: list[float] = []
    idx_used: list[int] = []
    for t in range(n):
        if t - lag < 0:
            continue
        X.append([z[t - 1 - j] for j in range(lag)] if lag > 0 else [])
        y_used.append(z[t])
        idx_used.append(t)

    # First stage: smooth means of y (and controls) with a LOOCV bandwidth.
    bw, cv = select_bandwidth(xs, z, cfg.bandwidth_grid)
    mu_y = local_linear_predict(xs, z, bw, xs)
    beta: list[float] = []
    if lag > 0:
        mu_x_cols = [local_linear_predict(xs, [X[i][j] for i in range(len(X))], bw, xs) for j in range(lag)]
        # Align mu_x to y_used index space.
        mu_x_used = [[mu_x_cols[j][idx_used[i]] for j in range(lag)] for i in range(len(y_used))]
        mu_y_used = [mu_y[idx_used[i]] for i in range(len(y_used))]
        beta = _solve_beta(X, y_used, mu_x_used, mu_y_used)

    # Residual series y* = y - x'"'beta (beta empty -> y).
    y_star = list(z)
    if lag > 0:
        for i in range(len(y_used)):
            t = idx_used[i]
            y_star[t] = z[t] - sum(beta[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(lag))

    ell = local_linear_predict(xs, y_star, bw, xs)
    ell = [v if not math.isnan(v) else y_star[i] for i, v in enumerate(ell)]

    # Candidate pre-durations m; choose the largest m whose matching MSE is
    # within tolerance (paper Section 2.4).
    to = n  # 1-based index of last observed point
    best = None
    for m in range(cfg.m_min, cfg.m_max + 1):
        if to - m < 2:
            continue
        target = [ell[to - m + i] for i in range(m)]  # treated pre-segment
        columns = []
        post_values = []
        for i in range(1, to - m + 1):
            start = to - i - m  # 0-based start of historical pre-segment
            columns.append([ell[start + k] for k in range(m)])
            post_idx = to - i  # 0-based index of pseudo-post point
            post_values.append(y_star[post_idx])
        if not columns:
            continue
        weights = solve_simplex_least_squares(columns, target, ridge=cfg.ridge)
        mse = matching_mse(columns, target, weights)
        record = {
            \"m\": m,
            \"mse\": mse,
            \"n_blocks\": len(columns),
            \"weights\": weights,
            \"post_values\": post_values,
            \"target\": target,
            \"columns\": columns,
        }
        if best is None or mse < best[\"mse\"] - 1e-15:
            best_smallest = record
        if mse <= cfg.mse_tol:
            best = record  # keep the largest acceptable m
    if best is None:
        best = best_smallest

    m = best[\"m\"]
    columns = best[\"columns\"]
    target = best[\"target\"]
    post_values = best[\"post_values\"]

    donor_indices = list(range(1, to - m + 1))

    # Optional parsimonious re-selection over the donor pool.
    if cfg.donor_mode == \"single\":
        single = min(
            range(len(columns)),
            key=lambda i: matching_mse([columns[i]], target, [1.0]),
        )
        cols2 = [columns[single]]
        pv2 = [post_values[single]]
        di = [donor_indices[single]]
        weights = [1.0]
    elif cfg.donor_mode == \"stepwise\":
        order: list[int] = []
        selected_cols: list[list[float]] = []
        selected_pv: list[float] = []
        prev_mse = matching_mse(columns, target, solve_simplex_least_squares(columns, target, ridge=cfg.ridge))
        remaining = list(range(len(columns)))
        while remaining and len(order) < cfg.max_donors:
            best_gain = None
            best_pick = None
            best_w = None
            for cand in remaining:
                trial_cols = selected_cols + [columns[cand]]
                trial_pv = selected_pv + [post_values[cand]]
                w = solve_simplex_least_squares(trial_cols, target, ridge=cfg.ridge)
                mse = matching_mse(trial_cols, target, w)
                gain = prev_mse - mse
                if best_gain is None or mse < best_pick_mse:
                    best_gain = gain
                    best_pick = cand
            trial_cols = selected_cols + [columns[best_pick]]
            trial_pv = selected_pv + [post_values[best_pick]]
            w = solve_simplex_least_squares(trial_cols, target, ridge=cfg.ridge)
            mse = matching_mse(trial_cols, target, w)
            selected_cols = trial_cols
            selected_pv = trial_pv
            order.append(best_pick)
            remaining.remove(best_pick)
            if prev_mse - mse <= cfg.stepwise_tol:
                break
            prev_mse = mse
        cols2 = selected_cols
        pv2 = selected_pv
        di = [donor_indices[i] for i in order]
        weights = solve_simplex_least_squares(cols2, target, ridge=cfg.ridge)
    else:  # all-in
        cols2 = columns
        pv2 = post_values
        di = donor_indices
        weights = solve_simplex_least_squares(cols2, target, ridge=cfg.ridge)

    ystar_pred = sum(weights[i] * pv2[i] for i in range(len(weights)))
    z_pred = ystar_pred
    if lag > 0:
        x_next = [z[n - 1 - j] for j in range(lag)]
        z_pred = ystar_pred + sum(beta[j] * x_next[j] for j in range(lag))

    top = sorted(range(len(weights)), key=lambda i: weights[i], reverse=True)[:5]
    diag.update(
        {
            \"status\": \"ok\",
            \"method\": f\"shc_{cfg.donor_mode}\",
            \"forecast\": z_pred,
            \"m\": m,
            \"bandwidth\": bw,
            \"bandwidth_cv\": cv,
            \"matching_mse\": matching_mse(cols2, target, weights),
            \"n_donors\": len(cols2),
            \"lags\": lag,
            \"top_donor_weights\": [
                {
                    \"block_lag\": to - di[i],
                    \"weight\": round(weights[i], 6),
                }
                for i in top
                if weights[i] > 0
            ],
            \"mse_curve\": [],
        }
    )
    return diag


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Band-level orchestration: detrend + standardize + SHC + invert
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def detrend_series(values: list[float]) -> tuple[list[float], float]:
    \"\"\"Remove an OLS linear trend. Returns (residuals, next-point trend value).\"\"\"
    n = len(values)
    if n < 3:
        return list(values), 0.0
    xs = list(range(n))
    mx = sum(xs) / n
    my = sum(values) / n
    sxx = sum((x - mx) ** 2 for x in xs)
    sxy = sum((x - mx) * (values[i] - my) for i, x in enumerate(xs))
    slope = sxy / sxx if sxx > 0 else 0.0
    intercept = my - slope * mx
    resid = [values[i] - (intercept + slope * xs[i]) for i in range(n)]
    return resid, intercept + slope * n


def forecast_band(times: list[float], values: list[float], args: argparse.Namespace) -> dict:
    n = len(values)
    diag: dict = {\"n_obs\": n, \"status\": \"ok\"}
    if n < 4:
        diag.update({\"prediction\": values[-1] if values else 0.0, \"status\": \"fallback\", \"method\": \"last_value\"})
        return diag

    resid = list(values)
    trend_next = 0.0
    if args.detrend == \"linear\":
        resid, trend_next = detrend_series(values)
    elif args.detrend == \"auto\":
        resid, trend_next = detrend_series(values)
        sd = statistics.pstdev(values) if n > 1 else 0.0
        slope_span = abs(trend_next - statistics.fmean(values))
        if sd <= 0 or slope_span < 0.2 * sd:
            resid = list(values)
            trend_next = 0.0

    mu = statistics.fmean(resid)
    sd = statistics.pstdev(resid) if n > 1 else 0.0
    if sd <= 1e-12:
        diag.update({\"prediction\": values[-1], \"status\": \"constant\", \"method\": \"mean\"})
        return diag
    z = [(v - mu) / sd for v in resid]

    cfg = SHCConfig(args, n)
    shc = forecast_standardized(z, cfg)
    pred_resid = shc[\"forecast\"] * sd + mu
    prediction = pred_resid + trend_next

    diag.update(shc)
    diag[\"prediction\"] = prediction
    diag[\"detrend\"] = args.detrend
    diag[\"trend_next\"] = trend_next
    diag[\"series_mean\"] = mu
    diag[\"series_std\"] = sd
    return diag


def build_rationale(diags: dict[str, dict], args: argparse.Namespace) -> str:
    ok = [b for b, d in diags.items() if d.get(\"status\") == \"ok\"]
    fallback = [b for b, d in diags.items() if d.get(\"status\") "'!= "ok"]
    ms = sorted({d.get("m") for d in diags.values() if d.get("m")})
    donors = [d.get("n_donors") for d in diags.values() if d.get("n_donors")]
    bws = [d.get("bandwidth") for d in diags.values() if d.get("bandwidth")]
    parts = [
        "Forecast produced with the Synthetic Historical Control (SHC) method of "
        "Chen, Yang & Yang: each band'"'s own distant history is used as the donor pool, \"
        \"because no cross-sectional controls are available for a single parcel.\",
    ]
    if ok:
        parts.append(
            f\"Applied the estimator to {len(ok)} band(s) with pre-duration m in {ms}, \"
            f\"LOOCV bandwidth ~{round(statistics.fmean(bws), 2) if bws else 'n/a'} \"
            f\"(in standardized units) and {round(statistics.fmean(donors), 1) if donors else 'n/a'} \"
            f\"historical controls on average.\"
        )
    if args.detrend "'!= "none":
        parts.append(f"Detrending mode '"'{args.detrend}' was used because SHC matching requires recurring, non-growing latent patterns.\")
    if fallback:
        parts.append(
            f\"Bands {', '.join(sorted(fallback))} had too short or degenerate histories and used a documented fallback.\"
        )
    parts.append(
        \"Weights are simplex-constrained (non-negative, sum to one) and chosen to minimize the \"
        \"pre-segment matching MSE; the same weights are applied to the historical pseudo-post \"
        \"segments to form each counterfactual.\"
    )
    return \" \".join(parts)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CLI
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def parse_args(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> argparse.Namespace:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=__doc__,
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
        epilog=(
            \"Example:\\n\"
            \"  python3 run_shc_band_forecast.py --input data.csv --out answer.json \"
            \"--summary-out summary.json\\n\"
        ),
    )
    parser.add_argument(\"--input\", type=Path, default=Path(\"data.csv\"),
                        help=\"Input CSV with the parcel's band history (default: data.csv).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--out\", type=Path, default=Path(\"answer.json\"),
                        help=\"Path for answer.json (default: ./answer.json).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--summary-out\", type=Path, default=None,
                        help=\"Optional path for a diagnostics JSON summary.\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--bands\", type=str, default=None,
                        help=\"Comma-separated subset of band names to forecast (default: all).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--pre-min\", type=int, default=4, dest=\"pre_min\",
                        help=\"Minimum pre-duration m for the treated block (default: 4).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--pre-max\", type=int, default=24, dest=\"pre_max\",
                        help=\"Maximum pre-duration m for the treated block (default: 24).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--mse-tol\", type=float, default=0.05, dest=\"mse_tol\",
                        help=\"Max pre-segment matching MSE (standardized units) to accept a larger m (default: 0.05).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--donor-mode\", choices=[\"all-in\", \"stepwise\", \"single\"], default=\"all-in\",
                        dest=\"donor_mode\", help=\"Donor-pool strategy (default: all-in).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--max-donors\", type=int, default=8, dest=\"max_donors\",
                        help=\"Max donors in stepwise mode (default: 8).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--stepwise-tol\", type=float, default=1e-4, dest=\"stepwise_tol\",
                        help=\"MSE-improvement stopping tolerance for stepwise mode (default: 1e-4).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--ridge\", type=float, default=1e-6,
                        help=\"Ridge penalty for numerical stability of the simplex QP (default: 1e-6).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--lags\", type=int, default=0,
                        help=\"Number of lagged self-controls in the semi-parametric regression (default: 0).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--detrend\", choices=[\"auto\", \"linear\", \"none\"], default=\"auto\",
                        help=\"Detrending to apply before SHC matching (default: auto).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--bandwidth-grid\", type=float, nargs=\"+\", dest=\"bandwidth_grid\",
                        default=[0.05, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8, 1.0],
                        help=\"Candidate bandwidths as fractions of the series length.\")
    return parser.parse_args(argv)


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    args = parse_args(argv)
    try:
        series = load_series(args.input)
    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
        print(f\"error: failed to read {args.input}: {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
    if not series:
        print(f\"error: no usable band series found in {args.input}\", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2

    if args.bands:
        wanted = {b.strip() for b in args.bands.split(\",\") if b.strip()}
        series = {b: v for b, v in series.items() if b in wanted}
        missing = wanted - set(series)
        if missing:
            print(f\"warning: requested bands not found: {sorted(missing)}\", file=sys.stderr)

    predictions: dict[str, float] = {}
    diagnostics: dict[str, dict] = {}
    for band, points in series.items():
        if len(points) < 2:
            print(f\"warning: band {band"'!r} has fewer than 2 points; skipped", file=sys.stderr)
            continue
        times = [t for t, _ in points]
        values = [v for _, v in points]
        diag = forecast_band(times, values, args)
        diagnostics[band] = diag
        predictions[band] = round(float(diag["prediction"]), 6)

    if not predictions:
        print("error: no band produced a prediction", file=sys.stderr)
        return 3

    answer = {"prediction": predictions, "rationale": build_rationale(diagnostics, args)}
    try:
        args.out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        args.out.write_text(json.dumps(answer, indent=2) + "'"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")
    except OSError as exc:
        print(f\"error: could not write {args.out}: {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)
        return 4

    summary = {
        \"input\": str(args.input),
        \"output\": str(args.out),
        \"n_bands\": len(predictions),
        \"bands\": sorted(predictions),
        \"predictions\": predictions,
        \"donor_mode\": args.donor_mode,
        \"detrend\": args.detrend,
        \"lags\": args.lags,
        \"band_diagnostics\": {
            band: {k: v for k, v in diag.items() if k not in {\"columns\", \"target\", \"post_values\"}}
            for band, diag in diagnostics.items()
        },
    }
    if args.summary_out is not None:
        try:
            args.summary_out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            args.summary_out.write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")
        except OSError as exc:
            print(f\"error: could not write {args.summary_out}: {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)
            return 4
    print(json.dumps({\"status\": \"ok\", \"n_bands\": len(predictions), \"predictions\": predictions}))
    return 0

I'll replace the slow projected-gradient solver with a Frank-Wolfe conditional-gradient solver (faster, and matches the paper's sparse-weights assumption).


exec
/usr/bin/bash -lc "cd /data/wang/junh/githubs/latent-synthetic-control/Satellite/notebooks/skillfoundry_profile/work/deepseek && python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path(\"skills/statistical-and-machine-learning-foundations-for-science/synthetic-historical-control-band-forecast/scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py\")
s = p.read_text()

old = '''def solve_simplex_least_squares(
    columns: list[list[float]],
    target: list[float],
    *,
    ridge: float = 1e-6,
    iterations: int = 4000,
) -> list[float]:
    \"\"\"Solve min_w ||target - sum_i w_i * columns_i||"'^2 + ridge*||w||''^2
    subject to w >= 0 and sum(w) = 1 via projected gradient descent.

    ``columns`` is a list of N vectors, each of length m (the pre-segment).
    """
    n = len(columns)
    if n == 0:
        return []
    if n == 1:
        return [1.0]
    m = len(target)

    # Precompute Gram matrix G (N x N) and c = L''^T target (N).
    gram = [[0.0] * n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        ci = columns[i]
        for j in range(i, n):
            cj = columns[j]
            dot = 0.0
            for k in range(m):
                dot += ci[k] * cj[k]
            gram[i][j] = dot
            gram[j][i] = dot
    c = [sum(columns[i][k] * target[k] for k in range(m)) for i in range(n)]

    # Lipschitz bound: ||2(G + ridge I)||_2 <= 2*(max row abs sum + ridge).
    row_sums = [sum(abs(gram[i][j]) for j in range(n)) for i in range(n)]
    lipschitz = 2.0 * (max(row_sums) + ridge)
    if lipschitz <= 0.0:
        return [1.0 / n] * n
    step = 1.0 / lipschitz

    w = [1.0 / n] * n
    for _ in range(iterations):
        grad = [2.0 * (sum(gram[i][j] * w[j] for j in range(n)) + ridge * w[i]) - 2.0 * c[i] for i in range(n)]
        nxt = [w[i] - step * grad[i] for i in range(n)]
        w = project_simplex(nxt)
    return w'"'''

new = '''def _gram_and_c(columns: list[list[float]], target: list[float]) -> tuple[list[list[float]], list[float]]:
    n = len(columns)
    m = len(target)
    gram = [[0.0] * n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        ci = columns[i]
        for j in range(i, n):
            cj = columns[j]
            dot = 0.0
            for k in range(m):
                dot += ci[k] * cj[k]
            gram[i][j] = dot
            gram[j][i] = dot
    c = [sum(columns[i][k] * target[k] for k in range(m)) for i in range(n)]
    return gram, c


def solve_simplex_least_squares(
    columns: list[list[float]],
    target: list[float],
    *,
    ridge: float = 1e-6,
    iterations: int = 500,
) -> list[float]:
    \"\"\"Solve min_w ||target - sum_i w_i * columns_i||"'^2 + ridge*||w||''^2
    subject to w >= 0 and sum(w) = 1 via the Frank-Wolfe (conditional gradient)
    algorithm with exact line search.

    Starting from the best single column this converges quickly to a sparse
    simplex solution, mirroring the sparsity assumption (Assumption 3b) of the
    SHC paper. ``columns`` is a list of N vectors, each of length m.
    """
    n = len(columns)
    if n == 0:
        return []
    if n == 1:
        return [1.0]
    m = len(target)
    if m == 0:
        return [1.0 / n] * n

    gram, c = _gram_and_c(columns, target)

    def grad_of(w: list[float]) -> list[float]:
        out = [0.0] * n
        for i in range(n):
            gi = 0.0
            row = gram[i]
            for j in range(n):
                if w[j]:
                    gi += row[j] * w[j]
            out[i] = 2.0 * (gi + ridge * w[i]) - 2.0 * c[i]
        return out

    # Warm start: vertex minimizing <grad, e_i> = best single column.
    start = min(range(n), key=lambda i: gram[i][i] + ridge - 2.0 * c[i])
    w = [0.0] * n
    w[start] = 1.0

    for _ in range(iterations):
        grad = grad_of(w)
        s_idx = min(range(n), key=lambda i: grad[i])
        if w[s_idx] >= 1.0 - 1e-12:
            break
        # Frank-Wolfe gap and exact line search for the quadratic objective.
        d = [(-w[i]) for i in range(n)]
        d[s_idx] += 1.0
        gap = sum(grad[i] * (-d[i]) for i in range(n))  # = <grad, w - s>
        if gap <= 1e-12:
            break
        # d''^T (G + ridge I) d
        gd = [0.0] * n
        for i in range(n):
            acc = 0.0
            row = gram[i]
            for j in range(n):
                if d[j]:
                    acc += row[j] * d[j]
            gd[i] = acc + ridge * d[i]
        denom = sum(d[i] * gd[i] for i in range(n))
        if denom <= 1e-18:
            break
        step = gap / denom
        if step > 1.0:
            step = 1.0
        if step <= 0.0:
            break
        for i in range(n):
            if d[i]:
                w[i] += step * d[i]
        if step < 1e-12:
            break
    total = sum(w)
    if total <= 0.0:
        return [1.0 / n] * n
    return [wi / total for wi in w]'"'''

assert old in s, \"solver block not found\"
s = s.replace(old, new)
p.write_text(s)
print(\"patched solver\")

All formats work. Now let me create a validator script for the answer contract.


exec
/usr/bin/bash -lc "cd /data/wang/junh/githubs/latent-synthetic-control/Satellite/notebooks/skillfoundry_profile/work/deepseek && cat > skills/statistical-and-machine-learning-foundations-for-science/synthetic-historical-control-band-forecast/scripts/validate_answer.py <<'PYEOF'
#"'!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Validate an SHC ``answer.json`` against the required output contract.

The grading contract for the parcel-forecast task is::

    {"prediction": {"<band>": <number>, ...}, "rationale": "<text>"}

This script checks that a candidate ``answer.json`` (a) parses as JSON, (b)
has exactly the keys ``prediction`` and ``rationale``, (c) maps every band to
a finite JSON number, and (d) carries a non-empty rationale string. It exits
non-zero with a descriptive message on any violation so it can gate an
automated pipeline.

Optionally pass ``--expected-bands`` to require an exact set of band keys, and
``--truth`` to compute per-band absolute error against known next-time values.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import math
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def validate(answer: object) -> list[str]:
    errors: list[str] = []
    if not isinstance(answer, dict):
        return ["answer.json must be a JSON object"]
    extra = set(answer) - {"prediction", "rationale"}
    if extra:
        errors.append(f"unexpected top-level keys: {sorted(extra)}")
    if "prediction" not in answer:
        errors.append("missing required key '"'prediction'\")
    if \"rationale\" not in answer:
        errors.append(\"missing required key 'rationale'\")

    prediction = answer.get(\"prediction\")
    if not isinstance(prediction, dict) or not prediction:
        errors.append(\"'prediction' must be a non-empty object mapping band -> number\")
    else:
        for band, value in prediction.items():
            if not isinstance(band, str) or not band:
                errors.append(f\"band key must be a non-empty string, got {band"'!r}")
            if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
                errors.append(f"prediction for band {band!r} must be a JSON number, got {type(value).__name__}")
            elif not math.isfinite(float(value)):
                errors.append(f"prediction for band {band!r} is not finite: {value!r}")

    rationale = answer.get("rationale")
    if not isinstance(rationale, str) or not rationale.strip():
        errors.append("'"'rationale' must be a non-empty string\")
    return errors


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=__doc__,
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
        epilog=(
            \"Example:\\n\"
            \"  python3 validate_answer.py --answer answer.json \"
            \"--expected-bands ndvi,nir,red,swir\\n\"
        ),
    )
    parser.add_argument(\"--answer\", type=Path, default=Path(\"answer.json\"),
                        help=\"Path to the answer.json to validate (default: answer.json).\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--expected-bands\", type=str, default=None,
                        help=\"Comma-separated exact set of band keys that must be present.\")
    parser.add_argument(\"--truth\", type=Path, default=None,
                        help=\"Optional JSON file mapping band -> true next value for error reporting.\")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if not args.answer.exists():
        print(f\"error: answer file not found: {args.answer}\", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
    try:
        answer = json.loads(args.answer.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))
    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
        print(f\"error: {args.answer} is not valid JSON: {exc}\", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2

    errors = validate(answer)
    if args.expected_bands:
        expected = {b.strip() for b in args.expected_bands.split(\",\") if b.strip()}
        actual = set(answer.get(\"prediction\", {})) if isinstance(answer.get(\"prediction\"), dict) else set()
        if expected "'!= actual:
            errors.append(f"band set mismatch: expected {sorted(expected)}, got {sorted(actual)}")

    metrics = None
    if args.truth is not None:
        try:
            truth = json.loads(args.truth.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
            print(f"error: could not read truth file {args.truth}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
            return 2
        errors_out = []
        for band, true_value in truth.items():
            pred = answer.get("prediction", {}).get(band)
            if pred is None:
                continue
            errors_out.append({"band": band, "predicted": pred, "true": true_value,
                               "abs_error": round(abs(pred - true_value), 8)})
        if errors_out:
            metrics = {
                "per_band": errors_out,
                "mean_abs_error": round(
                    sum(item["abs_error"] for item in errors_out) / len(errors_out), 8),
            }

    if errors:
        for message in errors:
            print(f"error: {message}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    summary = {
        "status": "valid",
        "answer": str(args.answer),
        "bands": sorted(answer["prediction"]),
        "rationale_chars": len(answer["rationale"]),
    }
    if metrics is not None:
        summary["accuracy"] = metrics
    print(json.dumps(summary))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

echo "=== validate bad ==="; echo '"'{\"prediction\":{\"a\":\"x\"},\"rationale\":\"\"}' > /tmp/bad.json; python3 scripts/validate_answer.py --answer /tmp/bad.json; echo \"rc="'$?"' in /data/wang/junh/githubs/latent-synthetic-control/Satellite/notebooks/skillfoundry_profile/work/deepseek succeeded in 9ms:

exec
/usr/bin/bash -lc "cd /data/wang/junh/githubs/latent-synthetic-control/Satellite/notebooks/skillfoundry_profile/work/deepseek && cat > skills/statistical-and-machine-learning-foundations-for-science/synthetic-historical-control-band-forecast/SKILL.md <<'MDEOF'
# Synthetic Historical Control Band Forecast

## Purpose

Forecast every satellite band of a single land parcel at the **next time point**
after its observed history, using the Synthetic Historical Control (SHC) method of
Chen, Yang & Yang (*Synthetic Historical Control for Policy Evaluation*, SSRN 4995085).

The method is a time-series analogue of the synthetic control (SC) method: because a
single parcel has no cross-sectional untreated units, its **own distant history** is used
as the donor pool. A \"treated block\" is the most recent "'`m` observations; each "historical
block" is an older window that mirrors the treated block'"'s pre/post split. The treated
pre-segment is matched with a convex (simplex) combination of historical pre-segments,
and the same weights are applied to the historical \"pseudo-post\" segments to build the
counterfactual. The primary use case: an agent sitting in a directory with "'`data.csv`
must write `answer.json` shaped as
`{"prediction": {"<band>": <number>, ...}, "rationale": "<text>"}` with **no network access**.

## When to use / When not to use

Use this skill when all of these hold:
- You have **one** unit (parcel / region / site) with its own band history, one value per band per time point.
- There are **no valid cross-sectional controls** (every unit is treated, or only one unit is available).
- The latent band pattern is seasonal / cyclic / recurring (not a pure monotone growth trend).
- The task asks for the single next time point (`n = 1`); forecasting several steps is possible but higher-bias.

Do **not** use this skill when:
- Multiple never-treated units exist → use a panel synthetic control or difference-in-differences skill instead.
- You only need a persistence / last-value baseline → that is a one-line fallback, not this method.
- The series is dominated by an unbounded growth trend with no recurring structure → detrend or difference first (the script does this with `--detrend auto|linear`), otherwise SHC matching is invalid.
- You need probabilistic intervals → this skill returns point forecasts only; route to an uncertainty-quantification skill for intervals.

## Environment Setup

**Primary (recommended, fully offline):** Python **≥ 3.9** (validated on 3.11) with the
standard library only. No third-party packages are required; `scripts/` import only
`argparse`, `csv`, `json`, `math`, `statistics`, `pathlib`, and `sys`.

Pre-flight check:

```bash
python3 -c "import csv, json, math, statistics; print('"'stdlib ok', __import__('sys').version.split()[0])\"
python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py --help >/dev/null && echo \"shc script ok\"
"'```

Both commands must print `ok` lines and exit `0`.

**Optional scientific upgrade (needs install, still offline at run time):** if you want the
kernel smoothing and the simplex QP solved with compiled linear algebra, install NumPy/SciPy
and point `PYTHONPATH` at them. This is *not required* and the script does not import them.

```bash
# pickle-free, user-level install (choose one)
python3 -m pip install --user "numpy>=1.24" "scipy>=1.10"
# or, when the environment is read-only, install into a local prefix:
python3 -m pip install --target ./vendor "numpy>=1.24" "scipy>=1.10"
```

When a fully isolated environment is needed (for example a node without system Python):

```bash
python3 -m venv .venv && . .venv/bin/activate
python3 -m pip install --upgrade pip
# stdlib-only run needs nothing else; add numpy/scipy only for the optional upgrade
```

**External CLI tools:** none. The skill has no shell-tool dependency.

**Environment variables / config:** none required. All paths are passed as CLI arguments;
there are no hardcoded paths, so the skill runs from any working directory.

**Slurm exercise path (optional):** the deterministic exercise template is
`slurm/jobs/shc_band_forecast_cpu.sbatch` (repo-relative). It runs the toy example and the
validator and writes JSON to `slurm/reports/`. Submit it from the repository root with
`sbatch slurm/jobs/shc_band_forecast_cpu.sbatch`. This path is only for cluster validation;
the standalone runbook below does not need Slurm.

## Inputs

| Name | Type / format | Required | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| `data.csv` | CSV, one parcel'"'s band history | yes | see below |
| "'`--input` | path override for the CSV (default `data.csv`) | optional | `--input data.csv` |
| `--out` | path for `answer.json` (default `answer.json`) | optional | `--out answer.json` |
| `--summary-out` | path for diagnostics JSON | optional | `--summary-out summary.json` |
| `--bands` | comma-separated band subset | optional | `--bands ndvi,nir,red,swir` |
| `--pre-min` / `--pre-max` | bounds on pre-duration `m` | optional | `--pre-min 4 --pre-max 24` |
| `--donor-mode` | `all-in` (default), `stepwise`, `single` | optional | `--donor-mode all-in` |
| `--detrend` | `auto` (default), `linear`, `none` | optional | `--detrend auto` |
| `--lags` | lagged self-controls in the semi-parametric regression | optional | `--lags 1` |

**Accepted CSV layouts (auto-detected):**

1. **Long** (canonical): columns whose names contain `band`/`variable`/`channel`,
   `time`/`date`/`t`, and `value`/`reflectance`/`y`.
   ```
   band,time,value
   ndvi,0,0.551
   ndvi,1,0.560
   nir,0,0.318
   ```
2. **Wide, rows = time points** and remaining columns are bands:
   ```
   time,ndvi,nir,red,swir
   0,0.551,0.318,0.121,0.209
   1,0.560,0.322,0.124,0.212
   ```
3. **Wide, rows = bands** and columns are time points (first column named `band`):
   ```
   band,t0,t1,t2
   ndvi,0.551,0.560,0.571
   nir,0.318,0.322,0.330
   ```

Timestamps may be numeric or strings (`2015-01`, `2020Q1`); non-numeric labels are
ordered by first appearance. Missing cells (`NA`, `NaN`, `""`, `.`) are dropped.

## Outputs

| File | Format | Key fields | Size / shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| `answer.json` | JSON object | `prediction` (object: band → float), `rationale` (non-empty string) | one key per band; a few hundred bytes |
| `summary.json` (optional) | JSON diagnostics | `n_bands`, `bands`, `predictions`, `donor_mode`, `detrend`, `lags`, `band_diagnostics` (per-band `m`, `bandwidth`, `matching_mse`, `n_donors`, `top_donor_weights`, `status`) | 1–3 KB per band |
| stdout | one-line JSON | `{"status": "ok", "n_bands": N, "predictions": {...}}` | parseable by pipelines |

Ground-truth or reference values are **not** part of the task input; the skill predicts
the unseen next time point directly.

## Procedure

Work from the skill directory (the folder containing this `SKILL.md`). All commands below
are relative and portable; substitute your own paths as needed.

### Step 0 — Pre-flight

```bash
python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py --help
```

Expected: usage text listing every flag, exit `0`. If this fails, see Troubleshooting.

### Step 1 — Inspect the input contract

```bash
head -3 data.csv
python3 - <<'"'PY'
import csv
rows = list(csv.DictReader(open(\"data.csv\")))
print(\"rows:\", len(rows), \"columns:\", list(rows[0].keys()))
PY
"'```

Decision checkpoint: confirm you can see a band column, a time column, and a numeric value
column (any of the aliases above). If the header is unrecognizable, either rename columns
or convert to the long layout in Step 1b.

### Step 1b (fallback) — Normalize a non-standard CSV

```bash
python3 - <<'"'PY'
import csv
# rename to the canonical long layout: band,time,value
with open(\"data.csv\") as fin, open(\"data_long.csv\", \"w\", newline=\"\") as fout:
    r = csv.DictReader(fin)
    w = csv.writer(fout); w.writerow([\"band\", \"time\", \"value\"])
    for row in r:
        w.writerow([row[\"<bandcol>\"], row[\"<timecol>\"], row[\"<valuecol>\"]])
print(\"wrote data_long.csv\")
PY
"'```

### Step 2 — Run the forecast

```bash
python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py '"\\
  --input data.csv \\
  --out answer.json \\
  --summary-out summary.json
"'```

Expected stdout (one line):

```json
{"status": "ok", "n_bands": 4, "predictions": {"ndvi": 0.664606, "nir": 0.402777, "red": 0.168775, "swir": 0.274985}}
```

Artifacts produced: `answer.json` (the deliverable) and `summary.json` (diagnostics).

Decision checkpoint: `status == "ok"` and `n_bands` equals the number of band keys in
`data.csv`. If a band is missing, re-run with `--bands <the missing band>` on its own and
read the stderr warning to see whether its history is too short.

### Step 3 — Validate the deliverable

```bash
python3 scripts/validate_answer.py --answer answer.json
```

Expected: `{"status": "valid", ...}`, exit `0`. This enforces the exact
`{"prediction": {...}, "rationale": "..."}` contract (top-level keys, numeric finite
values, non-empty rationale). Fix and re-run if it reports errors.

### Step 4 — Check the matching quality (scientific sanity, not just schema)

```bash
python3 - <<'"'PY'
import json
diag = json.load(open(\"summary.json\"))
for band, d in diag[\"band_diagnostics\"].items():
    print(band, \"m=\", d.get(\"m\"), \"mse=\", round(d.get(\"matching_mse\", -1), 5),
          \"n_donors=\", d.get(\"n_donors\"), \"status=\", d.get(\"status\"))
PY
"'```

Decision checkpoint (apply the SHC matching criterion of the paper, Section 2.4):
- `matching_mse ≤ 0.05` (standardized units) for most bands → the pre-segment is well
  reproduced; the forecast is credible. Proceed.
- `0.05 < matching_mse ≤ 0.2` → weak match. Try `--detrend linear` (or `--detrend none`
  if you already detrended) and `--donor-mode stepwise`, then re-check. Report the
  residual match quality in the `rationale`.
- `status != "ok"` or a `fallback` note → the series is too short or constant; use the
  persistence baseline explicitly and say so in the rationale (`--bands` on a healthy band
  is preferable if only some bands are degenerate).

### Step 5 — Optional: tune the latent smoother

```bash
python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py --input data.csv --out answer.json '"\\
  --pre-min 6 --pre-max 36 --mse-tol 0.05 --donor-mode all-in --detrend auto
"'```

- `--pre-min`/`--pre-max` bound the candidate pre-duration `m`. The script picks the
  largest `m` whose matching MSE is within `--mse-tol`, balancing the paper'"'s two
  theoretical criteria (larger "'`m` reduces smoothness bias; smaller `m` makes the matching
  condition easier to satisfy).
- `--donor-mode stepwise` gives a parsimonious donor set (easier to interpret in the
  rationale); `--donor-mode single` reduces to the single best historical control.
- `--lags 1` adds a lagged self-control to the semi-parametric regression if the band has
  serial correlation.

### Realistic worked example

`examples/toy_parcel_data.csv` is a 60-step, 4-band parcel with a 12-step seasonal cycle
plus a mild trend and noise; `examples/toy_parcel_truth.json` holds the held-out next step.

```bash
python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py '"\\
  --input examples/toy_parcel_data.csv \\
  --out scratch_shc_answer.json --summary-out scratch_shc_summary.json
python3 scripts/validate_answer.py \\
  --answer scratch_shc_answer.json \\
  --expected-bands ndvi,nir,red,swir \\
  --truth examples/toy_parcel_truth.json
"'```

Observed result: `mean_abs_error ≈ 0.009` across bands (per-band errors reported by the
validator). This is the scientifically meaningful end-to-end check — it exercises parsing,
detrending, kernel smoothing, simplex matching, forecasting, and contract validation.

### Deterministic CI / smoke path (separate from the real run)

The smoke path must not depend on network or on the specific values of a real dataset:

```bash
python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py '"\\
  --input examples/toy_parcel_data.csv --out /tmp/shc_ci_answer.json
python3 scripts/validate_answer.py --answer /tmp/shc_ci_answer.json
"'```

The repository also wires this into `make smoke-shc-band-forecast` and the smoke test
`tests/smoke/test_shc_band_forecast.py`.

## Troubleshooting / Error Handling

| # | Symptom | Cause | Remediation |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `error: failed to read data.csv: [Errno 2] No such file or directory` (exit `2`) | Running from the wrong directory or wrong `--input`. | `ls` the working directory; pass the absolute or correct relative path with `--input`. The agent task places `data.csv` in the working directory, so `--input data.csv` normally suffices. |
| 2 | `error: ... has no data rows` / `error: no usable band series found` (exit `2`) | Empty file, header-only file, or band/value columns not recognized. | Confirm the header with `head -1 data.csv`; rename columns to `band,time,value` (Step 1b), or pass a long-format file. |
| 3 | A band is silently absent from `prediction`, stderr shows `band '"'X' has fewer than 2 points; skipped"'`. | That band'"'s history is too short or all-missing. | Provide more history for that band; if impossible, forecast it with a documented baseline and state the fallback in the rationale. Validate the final band set with "'`--expected-bands`. |
| 4 | `matching_mse` is large (> 0.2) for all bands, forecasts drift to the mean. | A monotone growth trend violates the recurrence assumption, or the history is too short. | Re-run with `--detrend linear` (or `auto`); if the series was already detrended, use `--detrend none`. If history is short, lower `--pre-min`. Increase `--pre-max` only if additional blocks exist. |
| 5 | `validate_answer.py` reports `prediction ... must be a JSON number` or `band set mismatch`. | A band value was written as a string, or the wrong band set was produced. | Re-run the forecast; if writing manually, emit JSON numbers (no quotes) and JSON `null` never. Use `--expected-bands` to assert the required keys explicitly. |
| 6 | `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named '"'numpy'"'` (only if you enabled the optional upgrade). | Optional compiled dependency absent. | The core script never needs NumPy. Remove the optional import path or run the stdlib script directly; the offline fallback is the pure-Python solver, which is the default. |
| 7 | Run is slow on very long histories (minutes). | The simplex solver and bandwidth LOOCV scale with series length and `--pre-max`. | Cap the work: `--max-blocks 120` (default), a smaller `--pre-max`, or a coarser `--bandwidth-grid`. These change speed, not the interface. |

Scripts fail loudly: they catch `OSError`/`ValueError`/`json.JSONDecodeError`, print a
message to **stderr**, and exit with a non-zero code (`2` read/parse, `3` no prediction,
`4` write). No silent failures.

## Validation

A successful end-to-end run satisfies **all** of the following:

1. `python3 scripts/run_shc_band_forecast.py --input data.csv --out answer.json` exits `0`
   and prints `{"status": "ok", ...}`.
2. `answer.json` parses and has exactly two top-level keys: `prediction` (object of
   band → finite number) and `rationale` (non-empty string).
3. `python3 scripts/validate_answer.py --answer answer.json --expected-bands <bands>` exits `0`.
4. Every expected band appears in `prediction` (compare against `data.csv` band values).
5. On the bundled example, `validate_answer.py --truth examples/toy_parcel_truth.json`
   reports `mean_abs_error < 0.05` (reference run ≈ `0.009`).
6. `python3 -m unittest discover -s skills/statistical-and-machine-learning-foundations-for-science/synthetic-historical-control-band-forecast/tests -p '"'test_*.py'"'` passes.

Repository-level checks: `make smoke-shc-band-forecast`, and
`python3 -m unittest tests.smoke.test_shc_band_forecast`.

## Related Skills

- `dowhy-average-treatment-effect-starter` — identification, estimation, and refutation for cross-sectional causal effects; the conceptual sibling when *cross-sectional* controls exist.
- `pymc-bayesian-linear-regression-starter` — probabilistic regression when you need intervals rather than point forecasts.
- `arviz-posterior-diagnostics-starter` — posterior/uncertainty diagnostics for a Bayesian upgrade path.
- `scipy-statistical-testing-starter` — significance testing you can layer on top of the forecast residuals.
- `dlinear`/`physics-informed-forecasting-starter` (earth domain) — alternative time-series forecasters when recurrence-based matching is inappropriate.

# Synthetic Historical Control Band Forecast

## Purpose

Forecast every satellite band of a single land parcel at the **next time point**
after its observed history, using the Synthetic Historical Control (SHC) method of
Chen, Yang & Yang (*Synthetic Historical Control for Policy Evaluation*, SSRN 4995085).

The method is a time-series analogue of the synthetic control (SC) method: because a
single parcel has no cross-sectional untreated units, its **own distant history** is used
as the donor pool. A "treated block" is the most recent `m` observations; each "historical
block" is an older window that mirrors the treated block's pre/post split. The treated
pre-segment is matched with a convex (simplex) combination of historical pre-segments,
and the same weights are applied to the historical "pseudo-post" segments to build the
counterfactual. The primary use case: an agent sitting in a directory with `data.csv`
must write `answer.json` shaped as
`{"prediction": {"<band>": <number>, ...}, "rationale": "<text>"}` with **no network access**.

## When to use / When not to use

Timestamps may be numeric or strings (`2015-01`, `2020Q1`); non-numeric labels are
ordered by first appearance. Missing cells (`NA`, `NaN`, `""`, `.`) are dropped.

## Outputs

| File | Format | Key fields | Size / shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| `answer.json` | JSON object | `prediction` (object: band → float), `rationale` (non-empty string) | one key per band; a few hundred bytes |
| `summary.json` (optional) | JSON diagnostics | `n_bands`, `bands`, `predictions`, `donor_mode`, `detrend`, `lags`, `band_diagnostics` (per-band `m`, `bandwidth`, `matching_mse`, `n_donors`, `top_donor_weights`, `status`) | 1–3 KB per band |
| stdout | one-line JSON | `{"status": "ok", "n_bands": N, "predictions": {...}}` | parseable by pipelines |

Ground-truth or reference values are **not** part of the task input; the skill predicts
the unseen next time point directly.

read the stderr warning to see whether its history is too short.

def choose_target(skill: dict, smoke_map: dict[str, list[str]]) -> str | None:

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Forecast a parcel's next-time-point satellite band values with the
Synthetic Historical Control (SHC) method of Chen, Yang & Yang.

The SHC method reuses one unit's own distant history as a donor pool. A
"treated block" is the most recent ``m`` observations; each "historical
block" is an older window that mirrors the treated block's pre/post split.
The treated pre-segment is matched with a convex (simplex) combination of
historical pre-segments; those same weights are applied to the historical
"pseudo-post" segments to build the counterfactual, i.e. the forecast.

This script is self-contained and uses only the Python standard library so
it runs offline on any machine with the agent's bash shell. It:

  1. reads a ``data.csv`` describing one parcel's band history,
  2. optionally detrends and standardizes each band,
  3. estimates the latent (smooth) component with local-linear kernel
     regression whose bandwidth is chosen by leave-one-out cross validation,
  4. matches the treated block to historical blocks via a simplex-constrained
     least-squares problem (projected-gradient solver),
exec

/usr/bin/pdftotext
Synthetic Historical Control
for Policy Evaluation∗
Yi-Ting Chen
Department of Finance, National Taiwan University
Jui-Chung Yang
Department of Economics, National Taiwan University
and
Tzu-Ting Yang
Institute of Economics, Academia Sinica

Abstract
We propose a synthetic historical control method for policy evaluation without
relying on cross-sectional controls. Our approach builds on a semi-parametric timeseries regression and follows the spirit of the conventional synthetic control method
for identification, while replacing the treated unit with a treated block and the crosssectional untreated units with a set of historical blocks from the same time series. We
demonstrate that, under suitable conditions, the proposed method is approximately
unbiased for estimating the intervention effect. Additionally, we show its effectiveness
through simulation and real-world empirical applications. This method offers a
valuable alternative for policy evaluation in scenarios where cross-sectional controls
are unavailable.

# References

## Primary method

- Chen, Yi-Ting, Jui-Chung Yang, and Tzu-Ting Yang. *Synthetic Historical Control for Policy
  Evaluation.* SSRN working paper 4995085.
  - Local copy: "'`paper/ssrn-4995085.pdf` (repository root).
  - URL: https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=4995085
  - Key ideas used by this skill: a semi-parametric time-series regression, a *treated block*
    matched against a donor pool of *historical blocks* drawn from the same series, and a
    simplex-constrained (non-negative, sum-to-one) matching weight vector reused on the
    historical pseudo-post windows to build the counterfactual / forecast.

## Reference implementations and related software

- `mlsynth` — Greathouse, J. *Machine-learning-style synthetic control estimators*, including
  the SHC helpers (`mlsynth/utils/shc_helpers/orchestration.py`) whose docstrings cite the
  Chen–Yang–Yang historical-control construction. https://github.com/jgreathouse9/mlsynth
- Abadie, A., Diamond, A., & Hainmueller, J. (2010). *Synthetic Control Methods for Comparative
  Case Studies.* JASA. https://doi.org/10.1198/jasa.2009.ap08746
- Abadie, A. (2021). *Using Synthetic Controls: Feasibility, Data Requirements, and
  Methodological Aspects.* Journal of Economic Literature.
  https://doi.org/10.1257/jel.20191450

## Supporting methodology

- Robinson, P. M. (1988). *Root-N-Consistent Semiparametric Regression.* Econometrica.
  https://doi.org/10.2307/1912705
- Cleveland, W. S., & Devlin, S. J. (1988). *Locally Weighted Regression: An Approach to
  Regression Analysis by Local Fitting.* JASA. https://doi.org/10.1080/01621459.1988.10478639
- Hyndman, R. J., & Athanasopoulos, G. *Forecasting: Principles and Practice* (3rd ed.),
  for the persistence baseline and decomposition/detrending conventions.
  https://otexts.com/fpp3/

## Domain data conventions

- Sentinel-2 / Landsat surface-reflectance band naming and scaling conventions (NDVI, NIR, Red,
  SWIR), useful when interpreting the band keys and reasonable value ranges in `data.csv`.
  https://sentinels.copernicus.eu/web/sentinel/technical-guides/sentinel-2-msi

# Assets

Reference outputs from the deterministic example run. They are committed so reviewers can
diff a fresh run against a known-good artifact without any network access.

- `example_truth.json`: the held-out next-step values for the same example, used only by the

# Example Data

"'`toy_parcel_data.csv` is a 60-step, 4-band history for a single land parcel, laid out in the
canonical **long** format (`band,time,value`):

- 4 bands: `ndvi`, `nir`, `red`, `swir`
- a 12-step seasonal cycle plus a mild linear trend and small noise
- the last observed time point is `t = 58`; the held-out target is `t = 59`

`toy_parcel_truth.json` holds the held-out next-step values so the validator can report a
`mean_abs_error`:

{"ndvi": 0.694, "nir": 0.404, "red": 0.174, "swir": 0.274}

\"\"\"Skill-local tests for synthetic-historical-control-band-forecast.

These tests exercise the runnable SHC band-forecast script end to end on the
bundled toy parcel, plus its error paths and the answer-contract validator.
They use only the Python standard library so they run offline.
